- semantic_health_failures, SLO/error-budget series, telemetry_overhead_p95

**Required evidence**
- end-to-end trace reconstruction from user action to durable result
- redaction/cardinality/sampling/semantic-health regression tests

### L09 Performance, capacity & economics

**Implementation**
- Comparator: role-based access control with coarse service permissions
- Admission reserves scarce resources/cost before expensive work; saturation uses bounded queueing, routing, shedding, or rejection rather than uncontrolled degradation.

**Acceptance**
- Performance qualification includes tails and saturation, not median-only wins.
- Optimization dequalifies automatically when source/policy/profile identity becomes stale.

**Design invariants**
- Performance/cost claims bind exact workload, source, model/provider, hardware, runtime, concurrency, cache/warm state, scorer, and resource budget.
- Admission reserves scarce compute/memory/storage/network/cost before expensive work; saturation degrades via bounded queueing, routing, shedding, deferral, or denial.

**Failure modes**
- Median-only wins, hidden warm-cache bias, oversubscription, OOM, noisy-neighbor starvation, thrash, or stale profile evidence invalidate optimization claims.
- Any optimized path that loses correctness/security/privacy/authority/tenant isolation dequalifies regardless of speed or cost.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- latency_p50_p95_p99, throughput_at_fixed_SLO, saturation_knee, queue_wait_p95
- cost_per_qualified_outcome, memory/compute/utilization, cache_hit_rate where applicable

**Required evidence**
- paired baseline/candidate load profile with tails and confidence/variance
- resource-pressure and dequalification/fallback tests

### L10 Verification & adversarial evaluation

**Implementation**
- Focused test surfaces: skeleton/testing/test_safe_autonomy_bundle.py, skeleton/testing/test_blast_radius_alignment.py, skeleton/testing/test_human_control.py, skeleton/testing/test_autonomy_deescalation.py, skeleton/testing/test_agent_delegation_qualification.py, skeleton/testing/test_privileged_tool_transaction.py.
- Evaluation surfaces: .github/workflows/p1-safe-autonomy-bundle.yml.
- Verification must map each masterplan requirement, declared risk, trust boundary, concurrency invariant, and recovery obligation to deterministic tests or immutable evaluation evidence; every discovered failure becomes a versioned regression before promotion.

**Acceptance**
- Happy path, negative, adversarial, concurrency and recovery behavior are all exercised.
- High-impact verification is independent or deterministically separated from the generator/implementation under test.

**Design invariants**
- Every requirement, risk, trust boundary, concurrency invariant, recovery obligation, and superiority claim maps to deterministic tests or immutable evaluation evidence.
- High-impact verification is independent in role/evidence path; evaluator identity, dataset/workload, scorer, environment, seed/config, and contamination state are versioned.

**Failure modes**
- Judge leakage, test contamination, flaky required gates, self-promotion, missing negative cases, or scorer drift invalidates promotion evidence.
- Discovered failures become durable regressions with stable identities before the repaired behavior can be promoted.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- requirement_test_coverage, adversarial_failure_rate, flake_rate, evaluator_agreement/calibration
- regression_escape_rate, qualification_reproducibility, benchmark_uncertainty

**Required evidence**
- focused unit/integration/property/fuzz/adversarial/recovery suites
- immutable evaluation bundle with baseline, candidate, raw results, statistical method and verifier verdict

### L11 Deployment, migration & rollback

**Implementation**
- Deployment binds exact source, configuration, contracts, data migration, dependencies and artifact provenance.
- Canary/readiness gates observe correctness, SLO, safety, cost and compatibility before promotion; rollback/reference fallback is rehearsed.

**Acceptance**
- No irreversible production change lands without explicit migration/reconciliation semantics.
- Rollback restores a compatible authoritative state and is timed against the declared recovery target.

**Design invariants**
- Release artifacts are immutable and bind exact source, configuration, dependencies, contracts, schema/data migrations, SBOM/provenance, capability set, and enterprise evidence.
- Promotion proceeds through preflight, build, readiness, canary/smoke, monitored promotion, and rollback/failback criteria without hot production source mutation.

**Failure modes**
- Migration incompatibility, SLO/quality/safety/cost regression, provenance failure, or missing rollback target blocks or reverses promotion.
- Rollback preserves authoritative data compatibility or invokes explicit forward-reconciliation when data cannot safely move backward.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- deployment_success_rate, canary_regressions, rollback_success_rate, rollback_time_p95
- migration_duration/failures, provenance/SBOM coverage, release_evidence_freshness

**Required evidence**
- migration preflight + canary + rollback rehearsal on release-like topology
- artifact provenance/SBOM/signature and deployed-identity receipts

### L12 Operator controls & runbooks

**Implementation**
- Governance & Authority exposes health/readiness distinct from semantic correctness, plus bounded diagnostic/support evidence.
- Operator actions use preflight -> explicit admission -> execution -> immutable receipt; break-glass paths are scoped, expiring and auditable.

**Acceptance**
- On-call can identify owner, current state, dependency health, budget pressure, recent changes and recovery action.
- Administrative actions cannot silently bypass policy or evidence requirements.

**Design invariants**
- Operators have capability-scoped readiness, dependency health, saturation, recent-change, budget, queue, recovery, and evidence visibility without privileged-data leakage.
- Administrative and break-glass actions are explicit operations with scoped/expiring authority, preflight, two-person/approval policy where required, execution receipt, and postcondition verification.

**Failure modes**
- A green process health check cannot override semantic unready/degraded state; stale diagnostics cannot authorize destructive action.
- Support bundles and incident tooling remain tenant/redaction aware and cannot silently alter production state.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- MTTD/MTTR, operator_action_success/failure, readiness_transition_count, support_bundle_redaction_failures
- break_glass_uses, stale_health_rejections, incident_recurrence

**Required evidence**
- runbook/game-day exercising diagnosis, safe operator control, repair and rollback
- audit replay proving who authorized/executed what against which policy and state

### L13 Enterprise superiority & exit

**Implementation**
- Primary superiority outcome: versioned, delegable, revocable authority with decision evidence
- Dominance targets: privileged decision without policy/version evidence: 0 | delegation exceeding parent authority: 0 | revocation propagation p95: <=5s | authorization decision replayability: 100%

**Acceptance**
- Enterprise grade target is superior; current grade is designed.
- Legacy implementation status (implemented) cannot promote enterprise grade without dossier-conformant evidence.

**Design invariants**
- Enterprise grade is evidence-backed and monotonic only while governed source, contracts, policy, evaluator, workload, environment, and operating envelope remain current.
- A superior critical volume Pareto-dominates its declared baseline on required outcomes while meeting every non-compensable security/privacy/authority/tenant/state/recovery gate.

**Failure modes**
- Expired evidence, incomparable baseline, missing golden-journey coverage, open critical gap, or regression in a non-compensable dimension immediately invalidates enterprise/superior claims.
- Legacy implementation status, file count, LOC, prose, or self-scored benchmark can never substitute for qualification evidence.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- enterprise_qualification_freshness, dedicated_superiority_win_count, open_noncompensable_failures
- golden_journey_pass_rate, evidence_expiry/requalification_count

**Required evidence**
- exact-head enterprise dossier + superiority validator receipts
- applicable cross-plane golden-journey, comparator, rollback and independent-verifier evidence

**Evidence policy**
- Bind exact git head and governed source identity.
- Bind workload/dataset, model/provider where applicable, environment/hardware, policy/contracts, scorer, cost/resource budget, raw-result digest, and verifier verdict.
- Evidence expires when governed source, contract, policy, evaluator, or operating envelope changes.

**Completion rule:** Do not sign enterprise qualification from prose, file existence, LOC, happy-path tests, or legacy implementation status. Sign only after exact-head dossier requirements and enterprise gates pass.

## VOL-029 — Reliability

- Legacy implementation status: `implemented`
- Enterprise grade: `designed` → target `superior`
- Superiority profile: `ENT-VOL-029`
- Requirements: Use bounded retry/backoff/jitter, circuit breakers, bulkheads, queues, DLQ and load shedding as explicit policies. · Treat timeout as unknown outcome unless a postcondition proves failure/success. · Preserve availability without violating correctness or authority.
- Capabilities: reliability policy, failure isolation, backpressure/load shedding
- Contracts: `RetryPolicy`, `CircuitBreakerState`, `BulkheadPolicy`, `DeadLetterRecord`
- Canonical surfaces: `skeleton/reliability`, `skeleton/mesh/capacity_qualification.py`, `skeleton/ai/runtime/distributed/mesh/capacity_qualification.py`, `.github/workflows/p1-capacity-qualification.yml`
- Open obligations: standardize retry budgets across connectors · connect SLO/error-budget state to admission control

### L00 Intent & ownership

**Implementation**
- Reliability owns only the behavior declared by its masterplan requirements; it must not become a shadow owner for adjacent planes.
- Owned capabilities: reliability policy, failure isolation, backpressure/load shedding.

**Acceptance**
- All production entrypoints resolve to one canonical owner for VOL-029.
- Architecture validation rejects undeclared/shadow owners.

**Design invariants**
- Exactly one canonical production owner exists for the responsibility; compatibility facades may delegate but may not own policy, credentials, durable truth, or side effects.
- Declared capability scope is closed-world: undeclared behavior is rejected or routed through an explicitly declared adjacent-plane contract.

**Failure modes**
- Shadow-owner detection, duplicate durable authority, or competing policy evaluation is a release blocker.
- Ownership ambiguity during migration remains explicit transitional state with a single write authority and a dated removal/rollback plan.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- architecture_owner_resolution_failures_total = 0 at qualification
- shadow_owner_findings_open = 0 for production paths

**Required evidence**
- architecture/ownership validator output bound to exact head
- production entrypoint inventory proving canonical-owner convergence

### L01 Architecture & dependencies

**Implementation**
- Canonical implementation surfaces: skeleton/reliability, skeleton/mesh/capacity_qualification.py, skeleton/ai/runtime/distributed/mesh/capacity_qualification.py, .github/workflows/p1-capacity-qualification.yml.
- Zero-trust boundaries are enforced at the last responsible moment; tenant/user identity, purpose, classification, residency, and revocation follow every protected operation.

**Acceptance**
- Dependencies are declared, acyclic at the governed layer, and compatible with machine/architecture.json.
- A missing dependency or illegal edge fails before expensive/model work.

**Design invariants**
- Dependencies are explicit, versioned where externally visible, and cannot create a cycle that changes authority or startup order.
- Optional dependencies degrade capability explicitly; mandatory dependencies prevent readiness when unavailable or incompatible.

**Failure modes**
- Unknown dependency, incompatible version, or missing mandatory dependency fails before expensive/model/side-effect work.
- Partial dependency outage cannot silently convert an authoritative path into cache, projection, or compatibility fallback.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- dependency_resolution_failures by dependency and version
- capability-scoped readiness/degraded-state transitions

**Required evidence**
- dependency graph/fitness validation
- startup/degraded-mode tests for mandatory and optional dependencies

### L02 Contracts & compatibility

**Implementation**
- Primary contracts: RetryPolicy, CircuitBreakerState, BulkheadPolicy, DeadLetterRecord.
- Serialized/security-sensitive inputs use stable identity, version rules, canonical parsing, explicit absent/null semantics, and migration policy.

**Acceptance**
- Contract round trips and unknown/duplicate/malformed values are covered by negative tests.
- Compatibility policy defines forward/backward behavior and rejects unsafe ambiguity.

**Design invariants**
- Contract identity, schema version, canonical encoding, compatibility policy, absent/null semantics, and unknown-value behavior are stable and testable.
- Security-sensitive parsing rejects duplicate keys, non-finite values, ambiguous coercions, oversized fields, and unsupported versions before authority evaluation.

**Failure modes**
- Malformed or future-incompatible input fails closed with a stable normalized error and no side effect.
- Migration preserves identity/provenance and is reversible or explicitly reconciled when a perfect rollback is impossible.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- contract_decode_rejections_total by reason
- schema_migration_success/failure and compatibility-version telemetry

**Required evidence**
- round-trip, malformed, unknown-enum/version, duplicate-key and migration tests
- cross-runtime/canonical-serialization parity where multiple runtimes exist

### L03 Admission & authority

**Implementation**
- Use bounded retry/backoff/jitter, circuit breakers, bulkheads, queues, DLQ and load shedding as explicit policies.
- Principal/tenant, policy version, capability, budget, deadline and idempotency identity are attached before protected execution.

**Acceptance**
- Unauthorized or over-budget work consumes no privileged side effect.
- Fallback never weakens privacy, residency, authority, or tenant isolation.

**Design invariants**
- Principal, tenant/user binding, policy version, purpose, capability scope, budget, deadline, idempotency key, and data classification are known before protected work.
- Delegation can narrow but never widen authority; model output, retrieved content, tool results, network origin, and UI state cannot manufacture permission.

**Failure modes**
- Missing or ambiguous identity/policy/budget fails before provider/tool/state mutation.
- Fallback or retry reuses the original authority envelope and cannot relax privacy, residency, destructive-action, or tenant constraints.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- admission_denials_total by policy/budget/authority reason
- authority_decision_latency_p95 and stale_policy_rejections_total

**Required evidence**
- negative authorization/tenant/budget/deadline tests
- replay tests proving identical authority outcome for the same signed decision inputs

### L04 Runtime control flow

**Implementation**
- Treat timeout as unknown outcome unless a postcondition proves failure/success.
- Canonical sequence: normalize -> authorize -> reserve -> execute bounded capability -> persist/emit -> verify -> finalize receipt.

**Acceptance**
- Cancellation/deadline propagates through every child operation.
- No retry, recursion, delegation, search or background loop is unbounded.

**Design invariants**
- Runtime flow is a bounded state machine with explicit terminal states, monotonic operation identity, cancellation propagation, retry budget, concurrency cap, and child-work ownership.
- A model may propose next work but deterministic orchestration owns admission, transition, side-effect dispatch, checkpoint, and finalization.

**Failure modes**
- Crash/restart resumes from durable checkpoint or reconciles ambiguity; it never guesses success from missing acknowledgements.
- Cancellation, timeout, or budget exhaustion stops descendant work and prevents new privileged I/O after terminalization.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- operation_duration_p50_p95_p99, cancellation_latency_p95, retries_per_operation
- orphan_work_detected_total, budget_exhaustions_total, terminal_state_conflicts_total

**Required evidence**
- state-machine transition/property tests including illegal transitions
- crash/cancel/deadline/retry/fan-out boundedness tests

### L05 State & data semantics

**Implementation**
- Classify all Reliability data as authoritative, derived, cache, or ephemeral and assign one owner.
- Durable writes require transaction/outbox or explicit idempotency/compensation semantics; derived state is rebuildable where declared.

**Acceptance**
- Acknowledged authoritative writes are not silently lost.
- Retention/deletion/migration/rebuild semantics are executable for every persistent class used by the volume.

**Design invariants**
- Every record/store is classified authoritative, derived, cache, ephemeral, or recovery-only with one owner and explicit read/write semantics.
- Accepted durable mutations use transaction/outbox, compare-and-set/versioning, idempotency, or explicit compensation sufficient to make restart/replay semantics deterministic.

**Failure modes**
- Projection/cache failure cannot roll back accepted authoritative state unless the transaction contract explicitly couples them.
- Restore orders authoritative state before rebuildable projections; deletion/tombstones prevent resurrecting data outside lifecycle policy.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- authoritative_write_failures_total, outbox_backlog_age_p95, replay_reconciliation_total
- restore_rpo/rto, projection_lag_p95, deletion_propagation_lag_p95

**Required evidence**
- crash-before/after-commit and duplicate-replay tests
- backup/restore/rebuild/deletion/migration drills bound to authoritative state identity

### L06 Security, privacy & tenancy

**Implementation**
- Zero-trust boundaries are enforced at the last responsible moment; tenant/user identity, purpose, classification, residency, and revocation follow every protected operation.
- Threat/failure focus: retry storms | thundering herd | cascading failure | false failure after timeout.

**Acceptance**
- Critical authz, tenant, secret, injection and data-boundary failures are non-compensable.
- Logs/prompts/artifacts/telemetry contain no undeclared secret or protected data.

**Design invariants**
- Zero trust applies to user, model, retrieval, tool, file, network, plugin, provider, and worker input; validation occurs at the owning boundary and again at the last responsible moment before privileged effect.
- Tenant/user isolation, secret references, purpose limitation, residency, retention, egress, sandboxing, and least privilege survive caching, retries, derived artifacts, and fallback.

**Failure modes**
- Prompt injection, malicious retrieval/tool output, path/URL confusion, stale grants, cross-tenant identifiers, and secret-bearing payloads fail closed.
- Security/privacy/tenant regressions are non-compensable and cannot be approved merely because quality, latency, or cost improves.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- critical_security_boundary_violations = 0, cross_tenant_leaks = 0
- secret_redaction_failures = 0, denied_egress/authz attempts by reason

**Required evidence**
- adversarial injection/confused-deputy/tenant/secret/egress corpus
- threat-model-to-test traceability with all critical controls exercised

### L07 Failure, resilience & recovery

**Implementation**
- Open obligations: standardize retry budgets across connectors | connect SLO/error-budget state to admission control.
- Define timeout outcome semantics, retry budget, circuit/bulkhead/backpressure behavior, crash boundary, replay behavior, and recovery checkpoint for Reliability.

**Acceptance**
- Fault injection covers dependency down/slow, crash-before/after-commit, replay, saturation, cancellation, stale evidence/worker, and rollback.
- Recovery cannot create duplicate privileged effects or violate authority.

**Design invariants**
- Retries are bounded, jittered where appropriate, idempotency-aware, and combined with circuit breaking, bulkheads, backpressure, queue caps, deadlines, and explicit unknown-outcome handling.
- Recovery is deterministic from durable intent/checkpoint/evidence and stale workers/evidence cannot commit after fencing or source-identity invalidation.

**Failure modes**
- Dependency down/slow/partial, crash-before/after-commit, duplicate delivery, stale worker, split brain, saturation, cancellation, and rollback are injected as qualification scenarios.
- Unrecoverable or ambiguous state becomes durable reconciliation/incident work rather than silent success or blind retry.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- error_budget_burn, retry_amplification_factor, circuit_open_duration, queue_depth/age
- recovery_success_rate, recovery_time_p95, ambiguous_outcome_incidents_total

**Required evidence**
- fault-injection matrix covering every applicable architectural boundary
- recovery/replay/fencing tests proving no duplicate privileged effects

### L08 Observability & operability

**Implementation**
- Trace Reliability from operation identity through decisions, dependencies, state changes, evidence and terminal result.
- Emit structured metrics for success/failure, p50/p95/p99 latency where applicable, queue/resource pressure, retries, cost and recovery without hidden reasoning content.

**Acceptance**
- An operator can reconstruct a failed production operation without process-local memory.
- Telemetry has bounded overhead and tested redaction.

**Design invariants**
- One operation identity correlates logs, metrics, traces, provider/model routes, retrieval/memory/tool calls, state writes, costs, evidence, approvals, and terminal receipts.
- Telemetry is structured, bounded, redacted, tenant-aware, sampling-aware, and never depends on exposing hidden reasoning content.

**Failure modes**
- Missing correlation, secret/PII leakage, unbounded cardinality, or telemetry-induced outage cannot be masked by functional success.
- Semantic health is distinct from transport/liveness health; stale green probes cannot authorize risky work.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- trace_reconstruction_coverage, telemetry_drop_rate, redaction_failures, cardinality_budget
- semantic_health_failures, SLO/error-budget series, telemetry_overhead_p95

**Required evidence**
- end-to-end trace reconstruction from user action to durable result
- redaction/cardinality/sampling/semantic-health regression tests

**Implementation**
- Canonical implementation surfaces: skeleton/network/remote_execution.py, skeleton/ai/runtime/distributed/network/remote_execution.py, .github/workflows/p1-remote-worker-trust.yml.
- Model/provider objects terminate at the adapter boundary; routing/inference decisions bind model, configuration, budget, privacy, and terminal reason.

**Acceptance**
- Dependencies are declared, acyclic at the governed layer, and compatible with machine/architecture.json.
- A missing dependency or illegal edge fails before expensive/model work.

**Design invariants**
- Dependencies are explicit, versioned where externally visible, and cannot create a cycle that changes authority or startup order.
- Optional dependencies degrade capability explicitly; mandatory dependencies prevent readiness when unavailable or incompatible.

**Failure modes**
- Unknown dependency, incompatible version, or missing mandatory dependency fails before expensive/model/side-effect work.
- Partial dependency outage cannot silently convert an authoritative path into cache, projection, or compatibility fallback.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- dependency_resolution_failures by dependency and version
- capability-scoped readiness/degraded-state transitions

**Required evidence**
- dependency graph/fitness validation
- startup/degraded-mode tests for mandatory and optional dependencies

### L02 Contracts & compatibility

**Implementation**
- Primary contracts: Lease, FencingToken, MessageEnvelope, RemoteExecutionRequest.
- Serialized/security-sensitive inputs use stable identity, version rules, canonical parsing, explicit absent/null semantics, and migration policy.

**Acceptance**
- Contract round trips and unknown/duplicate/malformed values are covered by negative tests.
- Compatibility policy defines forward/backward behavior and rejects unsafe ambiguity.

**Design invariants**
- Contract identity, schema version, canonical encoding, compatibility policy, absent/null semantics, and unknown-value behavior are stable and testable.
- Security-sensitive parsing rejects duplicate keys, non-finite values, ambiguous coercions, oversized fields, and unsupported versions before authority evaluation.

**Failure modes**
- Malformed or future-incompatible input fails closed with a stable normalized error and no side effect.
- Migration preserves identity/provenance and is reversible or explicitly reconciled when a perfect rollback is impossible.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- contract_decode_rejections_total by reason
- schema_migration_success/failure and compatibility-version telemetry

**Required evidence**
- round-trip, malformed, unknown-enum/version, duplicate-key and migration tests
- cross-runtime/canonical-serialization parity where multiple runtimes exist

### L03 Admission & authority

**Implementation**
- Model partitions, ordering, leases/fencing, clocks, idempotency and consistency choices explicitly.
- Principal/tenant, policy version, capability, budget, deadline and idempotency identity are attached before protected execution.

**Acceptance**
- Unauthorized or over-budget work consumes no privileged side effect.
- Fallback never weakens privacy, residency, authority, or tenant isolation.

**Design invariants**
- Principal, tenant/user binding, policy version, purpose, capability scope, budget, deadline, idempotency key, and data classification are known before protected work.
- Delegation can narrow but never widen authority; model output, retrieved content, tool results, network origin, and UI state cannot manufacture permission.

**Failure modes**
- Missing or ambiguous identity/policy/budget fails before provider/tool/state mutation.
- Fallback or retry reuses the original authority envelope and cannot relax privacy, residency, destructive-action, or tenant constraints.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- admission_denials_total by policy/budget/authority reason
- authority_decision_latency_p95 and stale_policy_rejections_total

**Required evidence**
- negative authorization/tenant/budget/deadline tests
- replay tests proving identical authority outcome for the same signed decision inputs

### L04 Runtime control flow

**Implementation**
- Ensure stale workers cannot commit after lease loss.
- Canonical sequence: normalize -> authorize -> reserve -> execute bounded capability -> persist/emit -> verify -> finalize receipt.

**Acceptance**
- Cancellation/deadline propagates through every child operation.
- No retry, recursion, delegation, search or background loop is unbounded.

**Design invariants**
- Runtime flow is a bounded state machine with explicit terminal states, monotonic operation identity, cancellation propagation, retry budget, concurrency cap, and child-work ownership.
- A model may propose next work but deterministic orchestration owns admission, transition, side-effect dispatch, checkpoint, and finalization.

**Failure modes**
- Crash/restart resumes from durable checkpoint or reconciles ambiguity; it never guesses success from missing acknowledgements.
- Cancellation, timeout, or budget exhaustion stops descendant work and prevents new privileged I/O after terminalization.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- operation_duration_p50_p95_p99, cancellation_latency_p95, retries_per_operation
- orphan_work_detected_total, budget_exhaustions_total, terminal_state_conflicts_total

**Required evidence**
- state-machine transition/property tests including illegal transitions
- crash/cancel/deadline/retry/fan-out boundedness tests

### L05 State & data semantics

**Implementation**
- Classify all Distributed Systems data as authoritative, derived, cache, or ephemeral and assign one owner.
- Durable writes require transaction/outbox or explicit idempotency/compensation semantics; derived state is rebuildable where declared.

**Acceptance**
- Acknowledged authoritative writes are not silently lost.
- Retention/deletion/migration/rebuild semantics are executable for every persistent class used by the volume.

**Design invariants**
- Every record/store is classified authoritative, derived, cache, ephemeral, or recovery-only with one owner and explicit read/write semantics.
- Accepted durable mutations use transaction/outbox, compare-and-set/versioning, idempotency, or explicit compensation sufficient to make restart/replay semantics deterministic.

**Failure modes**
- Projection/cache failure cannot roll back accepted authoritative state unless the transaction contract explicitly couples them.
- Restore orders authoritative state before rebuildable projections; deletion/tombstones prevent resurrecting data outside lifecycle policy.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- authoritative_write_failures_total, outbox_backlog_age_p95, replay_reconciliation_total
- restore_rpo/rto, projection_lag_p95, deletion_propagation_lag_p95

**Required evidence**
- crash-before/after-commit and duplicate-replay tests
- backup/restore/rebuild/deletion/migration drills bound to authoritative state identity

### L06 Security, privacy & tenancy

**Implementation**
- Treat external/model/tool/retrieval/file inputs as untrusted until validated at the owning boundary.
- Threat/failure focus: split brain | clock/order confusion | ABA/stale lease | duplicate remote execution.

**Acceptance**
- Critical authz, tenant, secret, injection and data-boundary failures are non-compensable.
- Logs/prompts/artifacts/telemetry contain no undeclared secret or protected data.

**Design invariants**
- Zero trust applies to user, model, retrieval, tool, file, network, plugin, provider, and worker input; validation occurs at the owning boundary and again at the last responsible moment before privileged effect.
- Tenant/user isolation, secret references, purpose limitation, residency, retention, egress, sandboxing, and least privilege survive caching, retries, derived artifacts, and fallback.

**Failure modes**
- Prompt injection, malicious retrieval/tool output, path/URL confusion, stale grants, cross-tenant identifiers, and secret-bearing payloads fail closed.
- Security/privacy/tenant regressions are non-compensable and cannot be approved merely because quality, latency, or cost improves.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- critical_security_boundary_violations = 0, cross_tenant_leaks = 0
- secret_redaction_failures = 0, denied_egress/authz attempts by reason

**Required evidence**
- adversarial injection/confused-deputy/tenant/secret/egress corpus
- threat-model-to-test traceability with all critical controls exercised

### L07 Failure, resilience & recovery

**Implementation**
- Open obligations: remote execution protocol needs implementation | consistency choices need per-data-class documentation.
- Define timeout outcome semantics, retry budget, circuit/bulkhead/backpressure behavior, crash boundary, replay behavior, and recovery checkpoint for Distributed Systems.

**Acceptance**
- Fault injection covers dependency down/slow, crash-before/after-commit, replay, saturation, cancellation, stale evidence/worker, and rollback.
- Recovery cannot create duplicate privileged effects or violate authority.

**Design invariants**
- Retries are bounded, jittered where appropriate, idempotency-aware, and combined with circuit breaking, bulkheads, backpressure, queue caps, deadlines, and explicit unknown-outcome handling.
- Recovery is deterministic from durable intent/checkpoint/evidence and stale workers/evidence cannot commit after fencing or source-identity invalidation.

**Failure modes**
- Dependency down/slow/partial, crash-before/after-commit, duplicate delivery, stale worker, split brain, saturation, cancellation, and rollback are injected as qualification scenarios.
- Unrecoverable or ambiguous state becomes durable reconciliation/incident work rather than silent success or blind retry.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- error_budget_burn, retry_amplification_factor, circuit_open_duration, queue_depth/age
- recovery_success_rate, recovery_time_p95, ambiguous_outcome_incidents_total

**Required evidence**
- fault-injection matrix covering every applicable architectural boundary
- recovery/replay/fencing tests proving no duplicate privileged effects

### L08 Observability & operability

**Implementation**
- Trace Distributed Systems from operation identity through decisions, dependencies, state changes, evidence and terminal result.
- Emit structured metrics for success/failure, p50/p95/p99 latency where applicable, queue/resource pressure, retries, cost and recovery without hidden reasoning content.

**Acceptance**
- An operator can reconstruct a failed production operation without process-local memory.
- Telemetry has bounded overhead and tested redaction.

**Design invariants**
- One operation identity correlates logs, metrics, traces, provider/model routes, retrieval/memory/tool calls, state writes, costs, evidence, approvals, and terminal receipts.
- Telemetry is structured, bounded, redacted, tenant-aware, sampling-aware, and never depends on exposing hidden reasoning content.

**Failure modes**
- Missing correlation, secret/PII leakage, unbounded cardinality, or telemetry-induced outage cannot be masked by functional success.
- Semantic health is distinct from transport/liveness health; stale green probes cannot authorize risky work.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- trace_reconstruction_coverage, telemetry_drop_rate, redaction_failures, cardinality_budget
- semantic_health_failures, SLO/error-budget series, telemetry_overhead_p95

**Required evidence**
- end-to-end trace reconstruction from user action to durable result
- redaction/cardinality/sampling/semantic-health regression tests

### L09 Performance, capacity & economics

**Implementation**
- Comparator: stateless horizontal scaling with best-effort worker coordination
- Admission reserves scarce resources/cost before expensive work; saturation uses bounded queueing, routing, shedding, or rejection rather than uncontrolled degradation.

**Acceptance**
- Performance qualification includes tails and saturation, not median-only wins.
- Optimization dequalifies automatically when source/policy/profile identity becomes stale.

**Design invariants**
- Performance/cost claims bind exact workload, source, model/provider, hardware, runtime, concurrency, cache/warm state, scorer, and resource budget.
- Admission reserves scarce compute/memory/storage/network/cost before expensive work; saturation degrades via bounded queueing, routing, shedding, deferral, or denial.

**Failure modes**
- Median-only wins, hidden warm-cache bias, oversubscription, OOM, noisy-neighbor starvation, thrash, or stale profile evidence invalidate optimization claims.
- Any optimized path that loses correctness/security/privacy/authority/tenant isolation dequalifies regardless of speed or cost.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- latency_p50_p95_p99, throughput_at_fixed_SLO, saturation_knee, queue_wait_p95
- cost_per_qualified_outcome, memory/compute/utilization, cache_hit_rate where applicable

**Required evidence**
- paired baseline/candidate load profile with tails and confidence/variance
- resource-pressure and dequalification/fallback tests

### L10 Verification & adversarial evaluation

**Implementation**
- Focused test surfaces: skeleton/testing/test_remote_execution_protocol.py.
- Evaluation surfaces: .github/workflows/p1-remote-worker-trust.yml.
- Verification must map each masterplan requirement, declared risk, trust boundary, concurrency invariant, and recovery obligation to deterministic tests or immutable evaluation evidence; every discovered failure becomes a versioned regression before promotion.

**Acceptance**
- Happy path, negative, adversarial, concurrency and recovery behavior are all exercised.
- High-impact verification is independent or deterministically separated from the generator/implementation under test.

**Design invariants**
- Every requirement, risk, trust boundary, concurrency invariant, recovery obligation, and superiority claim maps to deterministic tests or immutable evaluation evidence.
- High-impact verification is independent in role/evidence path; evaluator identity, dataset/workload, scorer, environment, seed/config, and contamination state are versioned.

**Failure modes**
- Judge leakage, test contamination, flaky required gates, self-promotion, missing negative cases, or scorer drift invalidates promotion evidence.
- Discovered failures become durable regressions with stable identities before the repaired behavior can be promoted.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- requirement_test_coverage, adversarial_failure_rate, flake_rate, evaluator_agreement/calibration
- regression_escape_rate, qualification_reproducibility, benchmark_uncertainty

**Required evidence**
- focused unit/integration/property/fuzz/adversarial/recovery suites
- immutable evaluation bundle with baseline, candidate, raw results, statistical method and verifier verdict

### L11 Deployment, migration & rollback

**Implementation**
- Deployment binds exact source, configuration, contracts, data migration, dependencies and artifact provenance.
- Canary/readiness gates observe correctness, SLO, safety, cost and compatibility before promotion; rollback/reference fallback is rehearsed.

**Acceptance**
- No irreversible production change lands without explicit migration/reconciliation semantics.
- Rollback restores a compatible authoritative state and is timed against the declared recovery target.

**Design invariants**
- Release artifacts are immutable and bind exact source, configuration, dependencies, contracts, schema/data migrations, SBOM/provenance, capability set, and enterprise evidence.
- Promotion proceeds through preflight, build, readiness, canary/smoke, monitored promotion, and rollback/failback criteria without hot production source mutation.

**Failure modes**
- Migration incompatibility, SLO/quality/safety/cost regression, provenance failure, or missing rollback target blocks or reverses promotion.
- Rollback preserves authoritative data compatibility or invokes explicit forward-reconciliation when data cannot safely move backward.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- deployment_success_rate, canary_regressions, rollback_success_rate, rollback_time_p95
- migration_duration/failures, provenance/SBOM coverage, release_evidence_freshness

**Required evidence**
- migration preflight + canary + rollback rehearsal on release-like topology
- artifact provenance/SBOM/signature and deployed-identity receipts

### L12 Operator controls & runbooks

**Implementation**
- Distributed Systems exposes health/readiness distinct from semantic correctness, plus bounded diagnostic/support evidence.
- Operator actions use preflight -> explicit admission -> execution -> immutable receipt; break-glass paths are scoped, expiring and auditable.

**Acceptance**
- On-call can identify owner, current state, dependency health, budget pressure, recent changes and recovery action.
- Administrative actions cannot silently bypass policy or evidence requirements.

**Design invariants**
- Operators have capability-scoped readiness, dependency health, saturation, recent-change, budget, queue, recovery, and evidence visibility without privileged-data leakage.
- Administrative and break-glass actions are explicit operations with scoped/expiring authority, preflight, two-person/approval policy where required, execution receipt, and postcondition verification.

**Failure modes**
- A green process health check cannot override semantic unready/degraded state; stale diagnostics cannot authorize destructive action.
- Support bundles and incident tooling remain tenant/redaction aware and cannot silently alter production state.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- MTTD/MTTR, operator_action_success/failure, readiness_transition_count, support_bundle_redaction_failures
- break_glass_uses, stale_health_rejections, incident_recurrence

**Required evidence**
- runbook/game-day exercising diagnosis, safe operator control, repair and rollback
- audit replay proving who authorized/executed what against which policy and state

### L13 Enterprise superiority & exit

**Implementation**
- Primary superiority outcome: fenced distributed runtime with deterministic ownership, topology awareness, and recovery
- Dominance targets: split-brain authoritative commits: 0 | stale worker commits: 0 | fault-domain survival: >=99.9% qualified operations for declared fault set | distributed coordination overhead: <=10% p95 latency

**Acceptance**
- Enterprise grade target is superior; current grade is designed.
- Legacy implementation status (implemented) cannot promote enterprise grade without dossier-conformant evidence.

**Design invariants**
- Enterprise grade is evidence-backed and monotonic only while governed source, contracts, policy, evaluator, workload, environment, and operating envelope remain current.
- A superior critical volume Pareto-dominates its declared baseline on required outcomes while meeting every non-compensable security/privacy/authority/tenant/state/recovery gate.

**Failure modes**
- Expired evidence, incomparable baseline, missing golden-journey coverage, open critical gap, or regression in a non-compensable dimension immediately invalidates enterprise/superior claims.
- Legacy implementation status, file count, LOC, prose, or self-scored benchmark can never substitute for qualification evidence.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- enterprise_qualification_freshness, dedicated_superiority_win_count, open_noncompensable_failures
- golden_journey_pass_rate, evidence_expiry/requalification_count

**Required evidence**
- exact-head enterprise dossier + superiority validator receipts
- applicable cross-plane golden-journey, comparator, rollback and independent-verifier evidence

**Evidence policy**
- Bind exact git head and governed source identity.
- Bind workload/dataset, model/provider where applicable, environment/hardware, policy/contracts, scorer, cost/resource budget, raw-result digest, and verifier verdict.
- Evidence expires when governed source, contract, policy, evaluator, or operating envelope changes.

**Completion rule:** Do not sign enterprise qualification from prose, file existence, LOC, happy-path tests, or legacy implementation status. Sign only after exact-head dossier requirements and enterprise gates pass.

## VOL-031 — Compute & Hardware

- Legacy implementation status: `implemented`
- Enterprise grade: `designed` → target `superior`
- Superiority profile: `ENT-VOL-031`
- Requirements: Inventory CPU/GPU/RAM/VRAM/storage/network/NUMA/interconnect resources with freshness. · Admit/place work using hard resource constraints before optimization preferences. · Handle OOM, device reset, thermal throttling and heterogeneous hardware explicitly.
- Capabilities: hardware discovery, resource admission, placement inputs
- Contracts: `ResourceSnapshot`, `DeviceDescriptor`, `PlacementRequest`, `CapacityReservation`
- Canonical surfaces: `skeleton/network/model_placement.py`, `skeleton/ai/runtime/distributed/network/model_placement.py`, `.github/workflows/p1-model-placement.yml`
- Open obligations: hardware topology collector needs implementation · resource reservation must bind to scheduler

### L00 Intent & ownership

**Implementation**
- Compute & Hardware owns only the behavior declared by its masterplan requirements; it must not become a shadow owner for adjacent planes.
- Owned capabilities: hardware discovery, resource admission, placement inputs.

**Acceptance**
- All production entrypoints resolve to one canonical owner for VOL-031.
- Architecture validation rejects undeclared/shadow owners.

**Design invariants**
- Exactly one canonical production owner exists for the responsibility; compatibility facades may delegate but may not own policy, credentials, durable truth, or side effects.
- Declared capability scope is closed-world: undeclared behavior is rejected or routed through an explicitly declared adjacent-plane contract.

**Failure modes**
- Shadow-owner detection, duplicate durable authority, or competing policy evaluation is a release blocker.
- Ownership ambiguity during migration remains explicit transitional state with a single write authority and a dated removal/rollback plan.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- architecture_owner_resolution_failures_total = 0 at qualification
- shadow_owner_findings_open = 0 for production paths

**Required evidence**
- architecture/ownership validator output bound to exact head
- production entrypoint inventory proving canonical-owner convergence

- Performance/cost claims bind exact workload, source, model/provider, hardware, runtime, concurrency, cache/warm state, scorer, and resource budget.
- Admission reserves scarce compute/memory/storage/network/cost before expensive work; saturation degrades via bounded queueing, routing, shedding, deferral, or denial.

**Failure modes**
- Median-only wins, hidden warm-cache bias, oversubscription, OOM, noisy-neighbor starvation, thrash, or stale profile evidence invalidate optimization claims.
- Any optimized path that loses correctness/security/privacy/authority/tenant isolation dequalifies regardless of speed or cost.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- latency_p50_p95_p99, throughput_at_fixed_SLO, saturation_knee, queue_wait_p95
- cost_per_qualified_outcome, memory/compute/utilization, cache_hit_rate where applicable

**Required evidence**
- paired baseline/candidate load profile with tails and confidence/variance
- resource-pressure and dequalification/fallback tests

### L10 Verification & adversarial evaluation

**Implementation**
- Focused test surfaces: skeleton/testing/test_model_placement.py.
- Evaluation surfaces: .github/workflows/p1-model-placement.yml.
- Verification must map each masterplan requirement, declared risk, trust boundary, concurrency invariant, and recovery obligation to deterministic tests or immutable evaluation evidence; every discovered failure becomes a versioned regression before promotion.

**Acceptance**
- Happy path, negative, adversarial, concurrency and recovery behavior are all exercised.
- High-impact verification is independent or deterministically separated from the generator/implementation under test.

**Design invariants**
- Every requirement, risk, trust boundary, concurrency invariant, recovery obligation, and superiority claim maps to deterministic tests or immutable evaluation evidence.
- High-impact verification is independent in role/evidence path; evaluator identity, dataset/workload, scorer, environment, seed/config, and contamination state are versioned.

**Failure modes**
- Judge leakage, test contamination, flaky required gates, self-promotion, missing negative cases, or scorer drift invalidates promotion evidence.
- Discovered failures become durable regressions with stable identities before the repaired behavior can be promoted.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- requirement_test_coverage, adversarial_failure_rate, flake_rate, evaluator_agreement/calibration
- regression_escape_rate, qualification_reproducibility, benchmark_uncertainty

**Required evidence**
- focused unit/integration/property/fuzz/adversarial/recovery suites
- immutable evaluation bundle with baseline, candidate, raw results, statistical method and verifier verdict

### L11 Deployment, migration & rollback

**Implementation**
- Deployment binds exact source, configuration, contracts, data migration, dependencies and artifact provenance.
- Canary/readiness gates observe correctness, SLO, safety, cost and compatibility before promotion; rollback/reference fallback is rehearsed.

**Acceptance**
- No irreversible production change lands without explicit migration/reconciliation semantics.
- Rollback restores a compatible authoritative state and is timed against the declared recovery target.

**Design invariants**
- Release artifacts are immutable and bind exact source, configuration, dependencies, contracts, schema/data migrations, SBOM/provenance, capability set, and enterprise evidence.
- Promotion proceeds through preflight, build, readiness, canary/smoke, monitored promotion, and rollback/failback criteria without hot production source mutation.

**Failure modes**
- Migration incompatibility, SLO/quality/safety/cost regression, provenance failure, or missing rollback target blocks or reverses promotion.
- Rollback preserves authoritative data compatibility or invokes explicit forward-reconciliation when data cannot safely move backward.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- deployment_success_rate, canary_regressions, rollback_success_rate, rollback_time_p95
- migration_duration/failures, provenance/SBOM coverage, release_evidence_freshness

**Required evidence**
- migration preflight + canary + rollback rehearsal on release-like topology
- artifact provenance/SBOM/signature and deployed-identity receipts

### L12 Operator controls & runbooks

**Implementation**
- Compute & Hardware exposes health/readiness distinct from semantic correctness, plus bounded diagnostic/support evidence.
- Operator actions use preflight -> explicit admission -> execution -> immutable receipt; break-glass paths are scoped, expiring and auditable.

**Acceptance**
- On-call can identify owner, current state, dependency health, budget pressure, recent changes and recovery action.
- Administrative actions cannot silently bypass policy or evidence requirements.

**Design invariants**
- Operators have capability-scoped readiness, dependency health, saturation, recent-change, budget, queue, recovery, and evidence visibility without privileged-data leakage.
- Administrative and break-glass actions are explicit operations with scoped/expiring authority, preflight, two-person/approval policy where required, execution receipt, and postcondition verification.

**Failure modes**
- A green process health check cannot override semantic unready/degraded state; stale diagnostics cannot authorize destructive action.
- Support bundles and incident tooling remain tenant/redaction aware and cannot silently alter production state.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- MTTD/MTTR, operator_action_success/failure, readiness_transition_count, support_bundle_redaction_failures
- break_glass_uses, stale_health_rejections, incident_recurrence

**Required evidence**
- runbook/game-day exercising diagnosis, safe operator control, repair and rollback
- audit replay proving who authorized/executed what against which policy and state

### L13 Enterprise superiority & exit

**Implementation**
- Primary superiority outcome: explicit resource reservation, placement, eviction, warming, and locality
- Dominance targets: OOM due to admitted workload: 0 | resource utilization at fixed SLO: >=1.15x baseline | thrash/eviction instability: <=0.50x baseline events | unsafe in-flight eviction: 0

**Acceptance**
- Enterprise grade target is superior; current grade is designed.
- Legacy implementation status (implemented) cannot promote enterprise grade without dossier-conformant evidence.

**Design invariants**
- Enterprise grade is evidence-backed and monotonic only while governed source, contracts, policy, evaluator, workload, environment, and operating envelope remain current.
- A superior critical volume Pareto-dominates its declared baseline on required outcomes while meeting every non-compensable security/privacy/authority/tenant/state/recovery gate.

**Failure modes**
- Expired evidence, incomparable baseline, missing golden-journey coverage, open critical gap, or regression in a non-compensable dimension immediately invalidates enterprise/superior claims.
- Legacy implementation status, file count, LOC, prose, or self-scored benchmark can never substitute for qualification evidence.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- enterprise_qualification_freshness, dedicated_superiority_win_count, open_noncompensable_failures
- golden_journey_pass_rate, evidence_expiry/requalification_count

**Required evidence**
- exact-head enterprise dossier + superiority validator receipts
- applicable cross-plane golden-journey, comparator, rollback and independent-verifier evidence

**Evidence policy**
- Bind exact git head and governed source identity.
- Bind workload/dataset, model/provider where applicable, environment/hardware, policy/contracts, scorer, cost/resource budget, raw-result digest, and verifier verdict.
- Evidence expires when governed source, contract, policy, evaluator, or operating envelope changes.

**Completion rule:** Do not sign enterprise qualification from prose, file existence, LOC, happy-path tests, or legacy implementation status. Sign only after exact-head dossier requirements and enterprise gates pass.

## VOL-032 — High-Performance Native Core

- Legacy implementation status: `implemented`
- Enterprise grade: `designed` → target `superior`
- Superiority profile: `ENT-VOL-032`
- Requirements: Use native acceleration only behind stable capability/ABI boundaries with safe fallback. · Require memory/thread safety and deterministic error translation across FFI. · Version ABI and platform feature detection.
- Capabilities: native capability registry, FFI boundary, accelerated kernels
- Contracts: `NativeCapability`, `ABIManifest`, `NativeCallResult`
- Canonical surfaces: `skeleton/native`, `java-accelerators`, `skeleton/native/isolation.py`, `skeleton/native/selection.py`, `skeleton/ai/runtime/native/isolation.py`, `skeleton/ai/runtime/native/selection.py`, `scripts/profile_p2_acceleration.py`, `scripts/record_p2_acceleration_profile.py`

### L00 Intent & ownership

**Implementation**
- High-Performance Native Core owns only the behavior declared by its masterplan requirements; it must not become a shadow owner for adjacent planes.
- Owned capabilities: native capability registry, FFI boundary, accelerated kernels.

**Acceptance**
- All production entrypoints resolve to one canonical owner for VOL-032.
- Architecture validation rejects undeclared/shadow owners.

**Design invariants**
- Exactly one canonical production owner exists for the responsibility; compatibility facades may delegate but may not own policy, credentials, durable truth, or side effects.
- Declared capability scope is closed-world: undeclared behavior is rejected or routed through an explicitly declared adjacent-plane contract.

**Failure modes**
- Shadow-owner detection, duplicate durable authority, or competing policy evaluation is a release blocker.
- Ownership ambiguity during migration remains explicit transitional state with a single write authority and a dated removal/rollback plan.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- architecture_owner_resolution_failures_total = 0 at qualification
- shadow_owner_findings_open = 0 for production paths

**Required evidence**
- architecture/ownership validator output bound to exact head
- production entrypoint inventory proving canonical-owner convergence

### L01 Architecture & dependencies

**Implementation**
- Canonical implementation surfaces: skeleton/native, java-accelerators, skeleton/native/isolation.py, skeleton/native/selection.py, skeleton/ai/runtime/native/isolation.py, skeleton/ai/runtime/native/selection.py, scripts/profile_p2_acceleration.py, scripts/record_p2_acceleration_profile.py.
- State authority must distinguish durable truth from projection/cache/ephemeral data; projection loss must be recoverable from authority.

**Acceptance**
- Dependencies are declared, acyclic at the governed layer, and compatible with machine/architecture.json.
- A missing dependency or illegal edge fails before expensive/model work.

**Design invariants**
- Dependencies are explicit, versioned where externally visible, and cannot create a cycle that changes authority or startup order.
- Optional dependencies degrade capability explicitly; mandatory dependencies prevent readiness when unavailable or incompatible.

**Failure modes**
- Unknown dependency, incompatible version, or missing mandatory dependency fails before expensive/model/side-effect work.
- Partial dependency outage cannot silently convert an authoritative path into cache, projection, or compatibility fallback.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- dependency_resolution_failures by dependency and version
- capability-scoped readiness/degraded-state transitions

**Required evidence**
- dependency graph/fitness validation
- startup/degraded-mode tests for mandatory and optional dependencies

### L02 Contracts & compatibility

**Implementation**
- Primary contracts: NativeCapability, ABIManifest, NativeCallResult.
- Serialized/security-sensitive inputs use stable identity, version rules, canonical parsing, explicit absent/null semantics, and migration policy.

**Acceptance**
- Contract round trips and unknown/duplicate/malformed values are covered by negative tests.
- Compatibility policy defines forward/backward behavior and rejects unsafe ambiguity.

**Design invariants**
- Contract identity, schema version, canonical encoding, compatibility policy, absent/null semantics, and unknown-value behavior are stable and testable.
- Security-sensitive parsing rejects duplicate keys, non-finite values, ambiguous coercions, oversized fields, and unsupported versions before authority evaluation.

**Failure modes**
- Malformed or future-incompatible input fails closed with a stable normalized error and no side effect.
- Migration preserves identity/provenance and is reversible or explicitly reconciled when a perfect rollback is impossible.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- contract_decode_rejections_total by reason
- schema_migration_success/failure and compatibility-version telemetry

**Required evidence**
- round-trip, malformed, unknown-enum/version, duplicate-key and migration tests
- cross-runtime/canonical-serialization parity where multiple runtimes exist

### L03 Admission & authority

**Implementation**
- Use native acceleration only behind stable capability/ABI boundaries with safe fallback.
- Principal/tenant, policy version, capability, budget, deadline and idempotency identity are attached before protected execution.

**Acceptance**
- Unauthorized or over-budget work consumes no privileged side effect.
- Fallback never weakens privacy, residency, authority, or tenant isolation.

**Design invariants**
- Principal, tenant/user binding, policy version, purpose, capability scope, budget, deadline, idempotency key, and data classification are known before protected work.
- Delegation can narrow but never widen authority; model output, retrieved content, tool results, network origin, and UI state cannot manufacture permission.

**Failure modes**
- Missing or ambiguous identity/policy/budget fails before provider/tool/state mutation.
- Fallback or retry reuses the original authority envelope and cannot relax privacy, residency, destructive-action, or tenant constraints.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- admission_denials_total by policy/budget/authority reason
- authority_decision_latency_p95 and stale_policy_rejections_total

**Required evidence**
- negative authorization/tenant/budget/deadline tests
- replay tests proving identical authority outcome for the same signed decision inputs

### L04 Runtime control flow

**Implementation**
- Require memory/thread safety and deterministic error translation across FFI.
- Canonical sequence: normalize -> authorize -> reserve -> execute bounded capability -> persist/emit -> verify -> finalize receipt.

**Acceptance**
- Cancellation/deadline propagates through every child operation.
- No retry, recursion, delegation, search or background loop is unbounded.

**Design invariants**
- Runtime flow is a bounded state machine with explicit terminal states, monotonic operation identity, cancellation propagation, retry budget, concurrency cap, and child-work ownership.
- A model may propose next work but deterministic orchestration owns admission, transition, side-effect dispatch, checkpoint, and finalization.

**Failure modes**
- Crash/restart resumes from durable checkpoint or reconciles ambiguity; it never guesses success from missing acknowledgements.
- Cancellation, timeout, or budget exhaustion stops descendant work and prevents new privileged I/O after terminalization.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- operation_duration_p50_p95_p99, cancellation_latency_p95, retries_per_operation
- orphan_work_detected_total, budget_exhaustions_total, terminal_state_conflicts_total

**Required evidence**
- state-machine transition/property tests including illegal transitions
- crash/cancel/deadline/retry/fan-out boundedness tests

### L05 State & data semantics

**Implementation**
- State authority must distinguish durable truth from projection/cache/ephemeral data; projection loss must be recoverable from authority.
- Durable writes require transaction/outbox or explicit idempotency/compensation semantics; derived state is rebuildable where declared.

**Acceptance**
- Acknowledged authoritative writes are not silently lost.
- Retention/deletion/migration/rebuild semantics are executable for every persistent class used by the volume.

**Design invariants**
- Every record/store is classified authoritative, derived, cache, ephemeral, or recovery-only with one owner and explicit read/write semantics.
- Accepted durable mutations use transaction/outbox, compare-and-set/versioning, idempotency, or explicit compensation sufficient to make restart/replay semantics deterministic.

**Failure modes**
- Projection/cache failure cannot roll back accepted authoritative state unless the transaction contract explicitly couples them.
- Restore orders authoritative state before rebuildable projections; deletion/tombstones prevent resurrecting data outside lifecycle policy.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- authoritative_write_failures_total, outbox_backlog_age_p95, replay_reconciliation_total
- restore_rpo/rto, projection_lag_p95, deletion_propagation_lag_p95

**Required evidence**
- crash-before/after-commit and duplicate-replay tests
- backup/restore/rebuild/deletion/migration drills bound to authoritative state identity

### L06 Security, privacy & tenancy

**Implementation**
- Treat external/model/tool/retrieval/file inputs as untrusted until validated at the owning boundary.
- Threat/failure focus: undefined behavior | ABI drift | native crash corrupts process | silent numerical divergence.

**Acceptance**
- Critical authz, tenant, secret, injection and data-boundary failures are non-compensable.
- Logs/prompts/artifacts/telemetry contain no undeclared secret or protected data.

**Design invariants**
- Zero trust applies to user, model, retrieval, tool, file, network, plugin, provider, and worker input; validation occurs at the owning boundary and again at the last responsible moment before privileged effect.
- Tenant/user isolation, secret references, purpose limitation, residency, retention, egress, sandboxing, and least privilege survive caching, retries, derived artifacts, and fallback.

**Failure modes**
- Prompt injection, malicious retrieval/tool output, path/URL confusion, stale grants, cross-tenant identifiers, and secret-bearing payloads fail closed.
- Security/privacy/tenant regressions are non-compensable and cannot be approved merely because quality, latency, or cost improves.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- critical_security_boundary_violations = 0, cross_tenant_leaks = 0
- secret_redaction_failures = 0, denied_egress/authz attempts by reason

**Required evidence**
- adversarial injection/confused-deputy/tenant/secret/egress corpus
- threat-model-to-test traceability with all critical controls exercised

### L07 Failure, resilience & recovery

**Implementation**
- No legacy gap is recorded; enterprise evidence still remains mandatory.
- Define timeout outcome semantics, retry budget, circuit/bulkhead/backpressure behavior, crash boundary, replay behavior, and recovery checkpoint for High-Performance Native Core.

**Acceptance**
- Fault injection covers dependency down/slow, crash-before/after-commit, replay, saturation, cancellation, stale evidence/worker, and rollback.
- Recovery cannot create duplicate privileged effects or violate authority.

**Design invariants**
- Retries are bounded, jittered where appropriate, idempotency-aware, and combined with circuit breaking, bulkheads, backpressure, queue caps, deadlines, and explicit unknown-outcome handling.
- Recovery is deterministic from durable intent/checkpoint/evidence and stale workers/evidence cannot commit after fencing or source-identity invalidation.

**Failure modes**
- Dependency down/slow/partial, crash-before/after-commit, duplicate delivery, stale worker, split brain, saturation, cancellation, and rollback are injected as qualification scenarios.
- Unrecoverable or ambiguous state becomes durable reconciliation/incident work rather than silent success or blind retry.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- error_budget_burn, retry_amplification_factor, circuit_open_duration, queue_depth/age
- recovery_success_rate, recovery_time_p95, ambiguous_outcome_incidents_total

**Required evidence**
- fault-injection matrix covering every applicable architectural boundary
- recovery/replay/fencing tests proving no duplicate privileged effects

### L08 Observability & operability

**Implementation**
- Trace High-Performance Native Core from operation identity through decisions, dependencies, state changes, evidence and terminal result.
- Emit structured metrics for success/failure, p50/p95/p99 latency where applicable, queue/resource pressure, retries, cost and recovery without hidden reasoning content.

**Acceptance**
- An operator can reconstruct a failed production operation without process-local memory.
- Telemetry has bounded overhead and tested redaction.

**Design invariants**
- One operation identity correlates logs, metrics, traces, provider/model routes, retrieval/memory/tool calls, state writes, costs, evidence, approvals, and terminal receipts.
- Telemetry is structured, bounded, redacted, tenant-aware, sampling-aware, and never depends on exposing hidden reasoning content.

**Failure modes**
- Missing correlation, secret/PII leakage, unbounded cardinality, or telemetry-induced outage cannot be masked by functional success.
- Semantic health is distinct from transport/liveness health; stale green probes cannot authorize risky work.

**Telemetry & SLOs**
- trace_reconstruction_coverage, telemetry_drop_rate, redaction_failures, cardinality_budget
- semantic_health_failures, SLO/error-budget series, telemetry_overhead_p95

**Required evidence**
- end-to-end trace reconstruction from user action to durable result
- redaction/cardinality/sampling/semantic-health regression tests

- latency_p50_p95_p99, throughput_at_fixed_SLO, saturation_knee, queue_wait_p95
- cost_per_qualified_outcome, memory/compute/utilization, cache_hit_rate where applicable

**Required evidence**

- resource-pressure and dequalification/fallback tests
